  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:81:## When this discipline applies
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:82:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:102:1. **Detect drift** — probe `.claude/state/*.jsonl` for 
`any_drift: true` records that haven't been closed. Useful: `grep -l '"any_drift": true' .claude/state/*.jsonl | head` 
plus `tail -1 <file>` for latest state.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:103:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:104:2. **Pick highest-leverage drift** — per 
`.claude/rules/codification-threshold.md` ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE, drifts on root-level claims (CLAUDE.md) are 
higher-leverage than descendant claims (component CLAUDE.md files in `.claude/rules/`, `.claude/commands/`, etc.). Within 
a tier, prefer the most-cited claim.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:105:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:106:3. **Surgical edit** — apply the smallest diff that updates the 
count + adds a one-clause provenance line citing the SHA + cycle that introduced the drift. Use clause-prefixing pattern: 
new claim first, prior snapshot preserved as `Prior cycle-N snapshot:` sub-entry.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:107:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:116:## When NOT to apply
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:117:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:118:- **Single-fire saturation** — if 4+ drifts surface in one cycle, 
picking ALL of them violates ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE. Pick highest-leverage; defer rest.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:119:- **Audit script itself is broken** — if JSONL records are empty 
(e.g. `{"ts":"..."}` only) or use wrong field names, fix the audit before closing drifts based on its output.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:120:- **Drift is intentional** — sometimes a count claim is stale 
because it's awaiting a related ship. Don't close pre-emptively if the related ship is imminent (next 1-2 fires).
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\audit-action-loop.md:121:- **Cross-session ownership unclear** — if working tree shows 
uncommitted changes from a parallel session, prefer `git stash` + commit + restore over editing in-place; minimizes 
bundling risk.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\canonical.md:50:3. **Validate inputs and keep security checks intact.** At system 
boundaries only (user input, external APIs) — don't add defensive validation inside internal code (per CLAUDE.md hard 
rule).
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\canonical.md:51:4. **Prefer immutable updates over mutating shared state.** Judge state, 
rubric artifacts, eval cases — all new copies.

  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:35:7. **Anthropic-bundled-skill cites: cite the current slash 
command, not local folder names** — *(specialization of rule #4 for bundled-skill identifier divergence)* — Bundled skill 
identifiers can diverge across local folders, CCBP tables, changelogs, and official docs. Example: CCBP 
`changelog/best-practice/concepts/changelog.md:410 @ 76599cb` records a stale-name correction from 
`/fewer-permission-prompts` to `/less-permission-prompts`, while CCBP `best-practice/claude-skills.md:48 @ 76599cb` still 
lists the folder-style `fewer-permission-prompts`, AND the current official Commands page 
(`code.claude.com/docs/en/commands` line 90 [VERIFIED 2026-05-01 via codex T1 web-fetch]) shows 
`/fewer-permission-prompts` — so the three authoritative surfaces actively disagree. **Discipline**: when sss documents 
an Anthropic-bundled skill invocation, cite the current command from `code.claude.com/docs/en/commands` FIRST as the 
tie-breaker; use `code.claude.com/docs/en/skills` only for general skill mechanics; use CCBP tables/changelogs as 
secondary drift evidence when they conflict. Never treat `.claude/skills/<folder>` or plugin cache paths as proof of the 
user-visible slash command. Apply to: `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/agents/*.md`, `.claude/commands/**`, 
`.claude/skills/**/SKILL.md`, `.claude/rules/**`, `docs/sota-grand-catalog/**`, active adoption/citation docs, and commit 
bodies citing skill invocations. n=1 per `codification-threshold.md`: observed live conflict across official Commands 
docs / official Skills docs / CCBP changelog / CCBP skills table / sss docs costs ~15 min to re-derive per stale cite, 
this rule is <200 LOC; re-evaluate at the next Anthropic bundled-skill rename or official/CCBP conflict.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:36:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:37:8. **Composed-claims discipline + mechanical source-class 
reduction lattice** — *(Wave 17 fire-31 v1 codification per codex T1 fire-28 conf=0.87; UPGRADED at fire-36 per codex T1 
fire-33 NEEDS-REVISION on T2 b8fa7dd1 finding "weakest-source rule not computable" conf=0.72)* — When a single normative 
claim cites MULTIPLE source classes, the cite MUST declare:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:38:   - `constituents=[<class1@cite1>, <class2@cite2>, ...]` — 
explicit list of each constituent primitive at file:line + SHA OR official-docs URL
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:39:   - `effective_tier=<class>` — the EFFECTIVE class of the 
composed claim, computed by the reduction lattice below
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:40:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:48:   7. `TIER-1-DIRECT` (upstream verbatim file:line + SHA OR 
official-docs URL with anchor)
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:49:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:50:   **Reduction rule (mechanical)**: `effective_tier = 
MIN_PRECEDENCE(constituents)`. Concretely: if ANY constituent contains "TIER-3-LOCAL-*" OR the composition glue itself is 
local-codified (e.g., sss-novel composition pattern), the effective tier is `TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION` regardless of how 
many TIER-1 primitives are cited. EXCEPTION: if the composed claim is ONLY about an upstream primitive's EXISTENCE (e.g., 
"asyncRewake field exists" cited verbatim from Anthropic docs), it remains `TIER-1-DIRECT` because there's no local 
composition glue.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:51:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\citation-discipline.md:52:   **Anti-promotion rule**: composition CANNOT promote a 
TIER-2/TIER-3 component into TIER-1. The cite class of the COMPOSED claim is determined by the MIN_PRECEDENCE constituent 
OR by the composition-glue class, whichever is weaker.

variant and active-deep-research timeout both surface when `deep-review-exec` xhigh+danger-full-access profile is 
over-allocated for the task. The DEFAULT profile is structurally lighter — medium effort + read-only sandbox bypasses the 
failure modes that cause Pattern B HNF on focused single-claim audits. Pattern D is the **dispatch-shape preempting 
layer**; Pattern A is the **verdict-integration layer**; both honor cycle-300 ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE.

a Pattern B in-flight HNF. If Pattern B already fired and produced trace-mineable evidence, complete Pattern B per its 
disposition; use Pattern D for the NEXT T1 dispatch.

observability that catches mid-stream disconnect per gstack:1046-1048 mitigation pattern.

> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:432:## Mechanical-mirror exception — T1 SKIP for 
downstream-propagation ships (n=2 self-observed + n=1 user-triggered codification — same-session 2026-05-02)
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:433:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:434:T1 may be skipped only when the edit is pure 
downstream-propagation of a **settled upstream authority** to a sister/parallel surface (rule-layer ↔ skill-layer ↔ 
canonical-authority-surface). **Settled** means either (a) the upstream concept has terminal T1 APPROVE, or 
NEEDS-REVISION with all prescribed edits integrated, cited with `[VERIFIED via <verdict path>]` plus commit SHA, or (b) 
an explicit user-trigger directive is being copied as recorded authority with no interpretation. REJECT, INCONCLUSIVE, 
UNKNOWN, timeout-with-red-flags, or NEEDS-REVISION-with-open-prescriptions fail closed to fresh T1. This is a scoped 
mechanical-mirror exception, not a generic "looks mechanical" exception. T2 commit-time hook 
(`.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t2_pre_commit_gate.py:424` PreToolUse `Bash(git commit *)` per 
`.claude/rules/layered-gates-architecture.md` §5 Layer 2) is the cross-model verification net.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:435:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:436:### Activation predicates (ALL required)
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:437:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:440:3. **NO new authority/cite/status class introduced** — 
every new pointer has 1:1 cite to an already-settled rule/skill/command/script or explicit user-trigger authority; no 
fresh upstream source, new TIER-1/TIER-2/TIER-3 cite class, new hook-status claim, new verdict-confidence claim, new 
source-admissibility rule, or new canonical-owner claim is introduced.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:441:4. **Mirror chain context** — edit is part of a 
deliberate session-arc mirror cycle (n≥2 prior mirror ships within session) OR an explicit user-trigger directive points 
to an already-recorded authority/memory to mirror. User-trigger supplies trigger legitimacy only; it does not waive 
predicates #1-#3 or the settled-source requirement.
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:442:5. **Target-surface compatibility preflight** — before 
edit, read the source authority and the target section. If the pointer would contradict existing target semantics, 
require line-cite updates, retire or introduce FORWARD-REF/ACTIVE status flips, change local mechanics, or modify more 
than pointer placement, mechanical-mirror does NOT apply; fire fresh T1.

  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:454:### Anti-patterns
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:455:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:456:- **Predicate violated** (new mechanics inline / new 
authority-cite-status class introduced / unsettled source authority / target-surface contradiction) → mechanical-mirror 
does NOT apply; fire fresh T1 per Pattern A
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:457:- **Upstream verdict unresolved or negative** — source 
T1 verdict is REJECT, INCONCLUSIVE, timeout-with-red-flags, or NEEDS-REVISION with unapplied prescriptions; 
mechanical-mirror does NOT apply; STOP or fire fresh T1 per `.claude/rules/cross-model-consensus.md` §Verdict shapes

  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:34:# TIER-3 local codification provenance only: this rule was promoted

  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:35:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:36:When an agent (architect / codex T1 prescription / sota-researcher / 
Explore / general-purpose / codex-rescue / gpt5-reviewer) returns a ship-prescription with `prescribed_edits` / specific 
file:line claims about gaps to close, **pre-apply the Mia rule (verify against runtime state) BEFORE writing any Edit**. 
The agent's prescription IS NOT TRUSTED until verified — even when conf ≥ 0.90 + Pattern A trigger.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:37:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:38:The pattern: **AGENT reads brief context but DOES NOT probe runtime 
state → produces CATEGORY-CLAIM that LOOKS LIKE OPERATIONAL-CLAIM** (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Subclaim-type 
discriminator`). Agents systematically conflate "should be wired" with "is wired", "should be 80" with "is 80", "is 
unwired" with "is wired", without checking actual state via `git log` / `Grep` / `Glob`. This rule converts post-hoc OVER 
detection (existing `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` shape) into PRE-emptive verification (this rule's 
shape) BEFORE any Edit fires.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:39:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:67:3. **Verify EACH sub-claim**. If ≥1 sub-claim refuted → CATEGORY-CLAIM 
drift detected
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:68:4. **Classify a refuted gap-prescription as OVER and drop it.** 
Reserve HONEST-NON-FINDING for an agent/operator report that explicitly probed an assumed target and found no such target 
(per `synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories` OVER vs HNF distinction). Do NOT silently apply the prescription; 
document the refutation in commit body
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:69:5. **Apply only the verified-surviving prescriptions** in the single 
atomic Pattern A fix-forward commit per `.claude/rules/codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern A` — this rule is an 
admissibility filter BEFORE Pattern A's atomic-apply step (first drop OVER prescriptions, then apply all surviving 
verified prescriptions atomically)
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:70:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:71:## Empirical evidence ladder (n=29 cumulative — n=8 
same-arc-codification-basis + n=21 cross-arc post-codification)
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:72:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:107:ROI math: `Glob`/`Grep` probe ≈ 1-3s; reverting a wrong Edit ≈ 
5-15min (revert + verify + arc memory). Cheap probe pays for itself ~100× per refuted prescription. At n=3/session base 
rate this session, savings = ~15-45min/session × N sessions/mo = compounding.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:108:
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:109:The rule is **bidirectional** — saves false-OVER ship cycles (rows 4, 
6, 7) AND validates true-positive gap-claims (rows 5, 8). Pattern A admissibility filter shape: drop OVER, apply 
VERIFIED-GENUINE atomically.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:110:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:111:## Anti-patterns
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:112:
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:116:- **Apply prescriptions in single atomic batch without 
per-prescription verification** — refuted by Wave 13 same-arc evidence: codex T1 prescription #2 was OVER in a 
6-prescription batch; if I'd applied all 6 at once, the duplicate would have shipped. Per-prescription verification is 
necessary even when the agent batches.
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:117:- **Conflate OVER with HONEST-NON-FINDING** — these are distinct 
`synthesis-layer-verify.md §Reporting categories`: OVER = report claims more than git shows (refuted prescription class); 
HNF = report says "I probed for X, found nothing" (agent did the probe). A refuted gap-prescription where I probed and 
found the gap closed = OVER, NOT HNF (the agent didn't probe; I did).
> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:118:- **Apply Mia BEFORE codex T1 finishes** — Mia is a PRE-Edit gate; if 
codex T1 is still running, wait for verdict (Pattern A) OR honor Pattern B HNF disposition (timeout-without-verdict). DO 
NOT race Mia probe + Pattern A apply against an in-flight T1 — the T1 may correct or refute the Mia prescription itself 
(recursive: codex T1 ratifies Mia ratifies prescription).
  Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\mia-pre-apply.md:119:

when a tool output is truncated under budget, the emitter MUST label the snapshot (`[truncated at N tokens]`) AND name 
the recovery tool to fetch the full output (`call X with id Y for complete output`). Silent truncation forces the model 
to infer absence vs limit. Applies to `.claude/hooks/scripts/` printing to stderr under sys.exit(2) rewake and to Agent 
tool results exceeding `MAX_AGENT_OUTPUT`. (Prior cite to 
`awesome-agentic-patterns/patterns/context-window-auto-compaction.md:1` removed per codex T1 G-024 F-003 — that pattern 
covers context overflow auto-compaction, not truncated-output recovery pointers; the snapshot-labeling rule is 
sss-internal discipline.)

# Codex T1 single-fix-forward + timeout-trace-mining pattern

# TIER-3 local codification provenance only: promoted from
# feedback_iter15_21_codex_t1_arc_pattern.md (n=5 evidence) +
# feedback_codex_t1_timeout_extract_partial_evidence.md (n=3 evidence) per
# cycle-322 jurisdiction (n≥3 promotes feedback→rule). Feedback memories and
# codex_consult outputs are evidence trails, not SOTA design authority.

The codex T1 pre-edit consult discipline (per `cross-model-consensus.md`) produces
TWO distinct lifecycle outcomes that both honor the HARD GATE. This rule
codifies both with explicit branching disposition so neither becomes a
silent-fallback or false-failure mode.

## Pattern signature

```
                  ┌──────────────────┐
                  │ Build T1 consult │
                  │ (axes + options) │
                  └────────┬─────────┘
                           │
                           ▼
                  ┌──────────────────┐
                  │ Fire codex exec  │
                  │  --ephemeral     │
                  │  -p deep-review  │
                  │  background poll │
                  └────────┬─────────┘
                           │
              ┌────────────┴────────────┐
              ▼                         ▼
   ┌──────────────────┐      ┌──────────────────┐
   │ Pattern A        │      │ Pattern B        │
   │ JSON verdict     │      │ Timeout 240s,    │
   │ APPROVE or       │      │ no JSON, trace   │
   │ NEEDS-REVISION   │      │ contains evidence│
   │ + prescriptions  │      │ (or sandbox      │
   │                  │      │  blocked reads)  │
   └────────┬─────────┘      └────────┬─────────┘
            │                         │
            ▼                         ▼
   Apply ALL prescriptions    HONEST-NON-FINDING:
   in SINGLE atomic commit    mine trace, ship per
   (no iter-N→iter-N.1)       prior-fire research,
            │                  T3 post-commit verify
            ▼                         │
   T3 postcommit auto-fire ◄──────────┘
   verifies APPROVE
```

## Pattern A — NEEDS-REVISION → single fix-forward (n=5 promotion-eligible)

**Trigger**: codex T1 returns JSON verdict `NEEDS-REVISION` with confidence 0.88-0.93
and ≤10 `prescribed_edits`.

**Disposition**: apply ALL prescriptions in a SINGLE atomic commit per
`cycle-300` ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE. Do NOT iterate iter-N→iter-N.1→iter-N.2
— that's the `closed-loop-recursive-narrowing.md` cycle-539 layered-correction
anti-pattern. Verify via `git diff` per `synthesis-layer-verify.md` Mia rule
before commit.

**Why this works**: Codex deep-review profile is calibrated to find structural
defects in concrete proposals. Verdicts at conf 0.88-0.93 with 5-10
prescribed_edits = the "sweet spot" — high-confidence corrections, manageable
single-pass apply. The fix-forward applies cleanly and T3 postcommit (when
wired per iter-23 PostToolUse) typically returns APPROVE on the same commit.

**Empirical evidence trail (n=5)**:

| iter | T1 conf | prescriptions | result | commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| iter-18 (P4.b prepush port) | 0.91 | 10 | applied → no further fixes | 2a43eea |
| iter-19 (P5.a t1_gate port + wire) | 0.91 | 7 | applied → smoke probe 5/5 pass | 927532a |
| iter-20 (P6.a recursive P2 rescue) | 0.93 | 6 | applied → n-counter incremented | 2c74585 |
| iter-21 (P7.a sss activation) | 0.91 | 7 (selected_option=B) | applied → no further fixes | 9a09008 |
| iter-22 (P9 .mcp.json bootstrap) | 0.91 | 8 (selected_option=C) | applied → no further fixes | 1209786 |

Promotion-trigger satisfied at iter-22 (5th instance at conf 0.88-0.93 + ≤10
prescriptions). This rule IS the codification.

## Pattern B — T1 timeout-without-JSON-verdict → trace-mine + ship (n=3 evidence)

**Trigger**: codex T1 fired with valid 2-3-axis prompt but the 240s
`codex exec --ephemeral -p deep-review --color never` budget exhausts BEFORE
the agent emits a `{"verdict": ...}` JSON payload. Two known sub-causes:

1. **Active deep-research mode**: codex spends budget on web search +
   file reads investigating axes. Trace contains useful embedded evidence
   (URLs fetched, file:line citations) but no structured verdict.
2. **Sandbox-policy-blocked reads** (NEW-CLASS observed iter-26): codex T1
   runs with `sandbox=read-only` profile that rejects pwsh.exe-based file
   reads. Codex retries 2-3 pwsh patterns, all rejected, budget exhausts
   without substantive review.

**Disposition**: per TIER-3 local pattern provenance in `feedback_codex_t1_timeout_extract_partial_evidence.md`
HONEST-NON-FINDING reporting category (per `synthesis-layer-verify.md` §3
3-category framing): record T1 fired without verdict; mine trace for any
embedded line-cited evidence; proceed with ship per prior-fire research +
standing-directive defaults; T2 (`codex exec review --uncommitted`) and/or
T3 (postcommit auto via iter-23 wire) provide the cross-model verification
shifted to commit time.

**DO NOT loop iter-N→iter-N.1→iter-N.2 chasing a verdict** — the timeout
state is a signal about the prompt or sandbox, not the design. Per
cycle-907 standing directive (auto-proceed within authorized scope):
treat as fired-without-verdict, ship, verify on commit.

**Empirical evidence trail (n=3)**:

| iter | T1 prompt axes | timeout cause | trace evidence used | commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| iter-23 (T3+T4 wire) | 5 axes (broad) | active deep-research | upstream settings.json read, dependency import graph | db94b27 |
| iter-25 (env-var script) | 3 axes (security/scope/codex-auth) | active web-search of codex auth docs | none — codex was researching the right axis but never structured | eb6131f |
| iter-26 (T6 stop-gate port) | 2 axes (TIGHT) | sandbox blocked pwsh.exe reads (3 rejected) | none — codex never reached file content | af54fef |

n=3 evidence triggers cycle-322 promotion to rule layer (this rule). Pattern B
is now binding discipline alongside Pattern A.

## When this discipline applies

Both patterns apply to ANY /loop iter or substantive edit on a design surface
where codex T1 is fired. Gate path scope is default-in-scope and skips only
`_UNIVERSAL_EXCLUSIONS` per `.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t1_consult_gate.py:23-28,104-108,235`.
Common in-scope examples (NOT exhaustive):

- `.claude/hooks/scripts/*.py`
- `.claude/rules/*.md`
- `.claude/agents/*.md`
- `.claude/commands/*.md`
- `.claude/skills/**/SKILL.md`
- `.claude/schemas/*.json`
- `.claude/settings.json`
- `.mcp.json`
- `CLAUDE*.md`
- `PINS.json`
- `tools/sss.ps1`, `bin/sss.cmd`

Plus any single logical unit per cycle-300 ONE-LOGICAL-UNIT-PER-FIRE where
codex preflight (`_codex_preflight.py` per upstream) returns ok.

## How to apply

1. **Build T1 consult** at `.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>.txt`:
   - User directive verbatim
   - Context (prior fire findings + current state)
   - Proposed edit (or 2-3 options if uncertain — codex picks)
   - 2-3 explicit AXIS-N audit dimensions (TIGHT — see §Update triggers
     below for n=3 timeout learning)
   - Output schema (JSON-strict per `cross-model-consensus.md`)
   - Hint codex to use direct file reads (`rg`, `grep`) NOT
     `pwsh -Command` patterns (sandbox-blocked per Pattern B sub-cause 2)

2. **Fire codex T1 in background**:
   ```bash
   timeout 240 codex exec --ephemeral -p deep-review --color never \
     < .claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>.txt \
     > .claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt 2>&1 &
   ```

   **Pair-basename gate-compatibility note**: the prompt basename MUST equal the output basename before `_OUT`. `codex_t1_consult_gate.py:_consult_pairs()` strips `_OUT` from output filenames and looks for a prompt at exactly that stem. **`codex_consult_<topic>_PROMPT.txt` does NOT pair with `codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt`** — use canonical `<topic>.txt + <topic>_OUT.txt`. Prompts in `tmp/` are also invisible to the gate (it only globs `.claude/state/`). FM-08 root cause: my prompts in `tmp/iter*_t1_consult_*.txt` (gitignored) + verdicts in `.claude/state/<topic>_OUT.txt` produced n=14+ WARN false-positives across iters 63-66 because the prompt half of the pair was missing from `.claude/state/`.

3. **While T1 runs, do PREP**: read source files, smoke probes, design.

4. **Read T1 verdict** (search for `"verdict"` JSON key in OUT file tail):

   - **Pattern A path**: JSON verdict found at `verdict ∈ {APPROVE, NEEDS-REVISION, REJECT}`:
     - APPROVE conf ≥0.90 → ship as-proposed
     - NEEDS-REVISION conf ≥0.85 → apply ALL prescribed_edits in SINGLE commit
     - REJECT or conf <0.85 → STOP, escalate to user

   - **Pattern B path**: NO JSON verdict in tail (timeout, sandbox-blocked, etc.):
     - Mine trace for line-cited evidence (rg outputs, web URLs, file content)
     - Proceed with ship per prior-fire research + standing-directive defaults
     - Document trace evidence in commit body (which axes WERE substantively reviewed)
     - Save OUT file as audit-trail evidence

5. **Verify via `git diff`** per `synthesis-layer-verify.md` Mia rule before commit.

6. **Atomic commit** with body citing:
   - Verdict-on-file path (`.claude/state/codex_consult_<topic>_OUT.txt`)
   - Pattern (A or B) + evidence used
   - Prescription count (Pattern A) OR trace-evidence summary (Pattern B)

7. **T3 post-commit auto-verifies**: per iter-23 PostToolUse wire,
   `codex_postcommit_review.py` fires on commit and writes verdict to
   `.claude/state/codex_review_HEAD_<sha8>.txt`. Pattern B's "verification
   shifted to commit time" closes here.

## Why

**Pattern A** prevents the layered-correction anti-pattern: pre-iter-19,
median iters per converged ship was 3-5 (per
TIER-3 local pattern provenance in `feedback_codex_t1_mandatory_in_lifecycle.md`)
because each iter shipped fixes that codex T3 found defects in, escalating
across rounds. Pattern A collapses the iter chain into 2 steps (T1 + apply).

**Pattern B** prevents the false-failure anti-pattern: without explicit
trace-mining discipline, T1 timeouts feel like loop blockers and tempt
re-firing T1 with broader prompts (which makes timeouts MORE likely).
Pattern B reframes timeout as a known-handled mode where verification
shifts from T1 → T3 + prior-fire research, preserving cross-model consensus
without latency tax.

**Together**: across 8 ships in 2 /loop fires (iter-22→iter-26 + security
commit), 5 followed Pattern A and 3 followed Pattern B. All 8 produced
clean atomic commits. Median wall-time per ship ≈ 12-18 min including T1
fire-and-poll. Pre-pattern era: 3-5 iters × ~30 min = 90-150 min per
converged ship.

## When NOT to apply

- **Trivial typo fixes** (≤5 LOC delta, single-correctness change, no NEW cite class introduced, no NEW table row, no NEW rule body section, no semantic change to load-bearing claim) — skip T1. Tightened iter-N+6 per code-reviewer iter-25.1 P2-4 finding: iter-25 commit `544824c` rationalized 54-LOC SKILL.md addition with NEW Q2-2026 TIER-1 cite class as "trivial doc" — incorrect. Trivial = surgical-correctness fix (rename / cite-range correction / typo / inverted-count); NOT additive-new-content even if formally documentation. Apply T1 SYSTEM-meta-review fallback per `codex-t1-system-meta-review-fallback.md` if a prior verdict prescribes the change; otherwise fire fresh T1.
- **Bash-only operational commands** (no design surface to review) — skip T1.
- **Pattern A timeout escalation**: if codex T1 returns conf <0.85 OR REJECT,

> Z:\claude-sota\.claude\rules\codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md:434:T1 may be skipped only when the edit is pure 
downstream-propagation of a **settled upstream authority** to a sister/parallel surface (rule-layer ↔ skill-layer ↔ 
canonical-authority-surface). **Settled** means either (a) the upstream concept has terminal T1 APPROVE, or 
NEEDS-REVISION with all prescribed edits integrated, cited with `[VERIFIED via <verdict path>]` plus commit SHA, or (b) 
an explicit user-trigger directive is being copied as recorded authority with no interpretation. REJECT, INCONCLUSIVE, 
UNKNOWN, timeout-with-red-flags, or NEEDS-REVISION-with-open-prescriptions fail closed to fresh T1. This is a scoped 
mechanical-mirror exception, not a generic "looks mechanical" exception. T2 commit-time hook 
(`.claude/hooks/scripts/codex_t2_pre_commit_gate.py:424` PreToolUse `Bash(git commit *)` per 
`.claude/rules/layered-gates-architecture.md` §5 Layer 2) is the cross-model verification net.
